DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player controls a forward‑moving dot racing through a three‑lane tunnel, tapping to instantly shift the dot left or right (wrapping around) to line up with gaps and avoid obstacles. The satisfaction comes from rapid decision‑making and feeling the momentum of the tunnel while threading through ever‑changing openings.

## Player
Avatar: a small glowing dot representing the player's craft.
Verbs: shift left, shift right, (optional) pause. The dot continuously moves forward; only lane changes affect survival.

## Core loop
Every moment the tunnel scrolls forward, presenting a sequence of patterned gaps in each of the three lanes. The player watches the upcoming lane pattern, then taps left or right to move the dot into the safe lane before the gap reaches the dot. Successful alignment yields a reward and the tunnel continues; mistiming results in collision and loss.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- Static barriers: solid blocks that fill an entire lane segment, requiring the player to be in a different lane.
- Moving shutters: temporary closing walls that sweep across a lane and then retract, forcing quick lane changes.
- Gap traps: narrow openings that appear only briefly, encouraging precise timing.
All obstacles appear in any lane and may appear simultaneously in multiple lanes, but never block all three lanes at once.

## Score
- Each survived gap increments the score.
- Bonus points for chaining consecutive safe passes without shifting (maintaining momentum).
- Collectible pulses (optional) that appear in safe lanes grant extra points when the dot passes through them.

## Win and lose
The player loses when the dot collides with any barrier or fails to be in a lane with an opening at the moment the obstacle reaches the dot. The player wins by reaching a predefined distance or number of obstacles (e.g., the tunnel's end) and a final "victory" screen appears, showing the score and a prompt to restart.

## Feel
Fast‑paced, rhythm‑driven arcade action with a neon‑glow aesthetic. The tunnel glows with pulsing colors and the dot leaves a subtle trailing light. Sound consists of crisp electronic beeps for lane shifts and satisfying chimes for successful passes, creating a tense yet upbeat atmosphere.

## Why it's fun
The thrill of instantly swapping lanes to thread a moving dot through ever‑shifting gaps generates those “got‑me!” moments that make players grin or curse in equal measure.

TASKS.md

show
1. Scaffold HTML and meta.json — create `index.html` with required header, canvas, restart button, and an empty script block; generate `meta.json` matching the game title, description, controls, and tags.
2. Define initial game state — declare player object, hazards array, score, and phase/state literals at the top of the script with default values.
3. Set up animation loop — implement `startLoop` with `cancelAnimationFrame` handling, requestAnimationFrame loop, and canvas clearing; wrap loop body in try/catch.
4. Input handling — add a `keys` Set, `keydown`/`keyup` listeners, R-key restart hook, and click listener on the restart button that both call a common `reset` function.
5. Player movement — implement dt‑based lane shifting based on input; update player position each frame and ensure lane wrap‑around.
6. Hazard spawning and movement — create hazard objects, spawn them respecting `SAFE_RADIUS` so none appear on the player within the first 3 seconds, and move them each frame scaled by dt.
7. Collision detection and game over — check player vs hazards each frame, transition `state` to "gameover" on hit, and stop the active gameplay loop.
8. Score system — increase score on each survived obstacle (or safe pass), draw the current score on the canvas top‑left.
9. Rendering — clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player (high‑contrast), and HUD elements each frame.
10. Game‑over overlay — dim the background, render "Game Over", final score, and "Press R or click Restart" instructions.
11. Observable state contract — populate `window.__game` each frame with `state`, `score`, `player` coordinates, visibility flag, and a concise `objective` sentence.
12. Audio integration — add a lazy‑initialized `AudioContext` in a `beep()` helper; trigger sounds on lane shift, scoring events, and death, all wrapped in try/catch.

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Lane Shift</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Navigate a dot through a three‑lane tunnel, shifting lanes instantly to avoid obstacles.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Tap left or right to shift lanes (wrapping around)</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart to restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  // Game configuration constants
  const PLAYER_SPEED = 200; // pixels per second, forward movement
  const LANE_COUNT = 3; // number of lanes in the tunnel
  const SAFE_RADIUS = PLAYER_SPEED * 3; // safe distance (3 seconds)
  const HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL = 1.5; // seconds between hazard spawns
  const HAZARD_SPEED = PLAYER_SPEED; // speed hazards move relative to player

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  // Canvas and context
  const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
  const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');

  // Game state objects – initialized with default values
  const player = {
    // lane index: 0 (left), 1 (center), 2 (right)
    lane: 1,
    // continuous forward position (pixels) – starts at left edge
    x: 0,
    // vertical position (pixels) – fixed for this simple scaffold
    y: canvas.height / 2,
    // visible flag for observable contract
    visible: true,
  };

  // Array of active hazards/obstacles – empty at start
  const hazards = [];

  // Score counter
  let score = 0;

  // Phase / state literal – possible values: "playing", "gameover", "paused"
  let phase = 'playing';

  // Placeholder variables for future use (timers, loop control, etc.)
  let lastTime = performance.now();
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  // === RESET ===============================================================
  // Reset game state to defaults
  function reset() {
    // Reset player position and lane
    player.lane = 1;
    player.x = 0;
    player.y = canvas.height / 2;
    player.visible = true;
    // Clear hazards
    hazards.length = 0;
    // Reset score and phase
    score = 0;
    phase = 'playing';
    // Reset timing
    lastTime = performance.now();
    // Publish initial state
    publishState();
    // Start animation loop
    startLoop();
  }

  // === LOOP / UPDATE ======================================================
  // Animation loop implementation
  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        // Clear canvas each frame
        ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
        // Publish state after each tick (optional but keeps observable up‑to‑date)
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error('loop error', err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  // No rendering yet – scaffold only

  // === INPUT ===============================================================
  // Input handling via keys Set and listeners
  const keys = new Set();
  document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
    // Restart on R key
    if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
    keys.delete(ev.key);
  });
  // Click restart button also resets the game
  restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  // No audio yet – scaffold only

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    // Publish a minimal observable state for external tools
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,
      score,
      player: {
        lane: player.lane,
        x: player.x,
        y: player.y,
        visible: player.visible,
      },
      objective: 'Shift lanes to avoid obstacles.',
    };
  }

  // Publish initial state on load
  publishState();

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  // No bootstrap actions required for scaffold
  </script>
</body>
</html>

On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. - "canvas appears blank" → the game is loading but `render()` isn't drawing anything. Check that `startLoop()` actually got called at boot, that `render()` runs every frame, and that you're issuing `ctx.fill*` / `ctx.stroke*` calls per entity each frame (not just clearing the canvas).

prompt (12,576 chars)
## Reference example: "snake"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Snake",
  "description": "Eat the food. Don't bite yourself or hit the walls. The snake speeds up as you grow.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or WASD to turn",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "snake", "grid"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Snake</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; image-rendering: pixelated; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Snake</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Eat the food. Don't bite yourself or hit the walls. The snake speeds up as you grow.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or WASD to turn</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const CELL = 25;
  const COLS = 32;            // 32 * 25 = 800
  const ROWS = 24;            // 24 * 25 = 600
  const TICK_START_MS = 140;  // snake step interval at game start
  const TICK_MIN_MS = 60;     // floor speed
  const TICK_SPEEDUP = 3;     // ms shaved off per food eaten

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let snake;          // array of { x, y } in grid coords; snake[0] is head
  let dir;            // { x, y } unit vector
  let nextDir;        // queued direction (so quick double-taps don't U-turn)
  let food;           // { x, y } in grid coords
  let score;
  let highScore = 0;
  let stepIntervalMs;
  let stepAccumulatorMs;
  let phase;          // "playing" | "gameover"
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    snake = [{ x: 10, y: 12 }, { x: 9, y: 12 }, { x: 8, y: 12 }];
    dir = { x: 1, y: 0 };
    nextDir = dir;
    food = randomFreeCell();
    score = 0;
    stepIntervalMs = TICK_START_MS;
    stepAccumulatorMs = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    stepAccumulatorMs += dt * 1000;
    while (stepAccumulatorMs >= stepIntervalMs) {
      stepAccumulatorMs -= stepIntervalMs;
      step();
      if (phase !== "playing") return;
    }
  }

  function step() {
    // Commit queued direction; reject reversals.
    if (nextDir.x !== -dir.x || nextDir.y !== -dir.y) dir = nextDir;

    const head = snake[0];
    const nx = head.x + dir.x;
    const ny = head.y + dir.y;

    // Wall collision -> game over.
    if (nx < 0 || nx >= COLS || ny < 0 || ny >= ROWS) {
      gameOver();
      return;
    }
    // Self collision (skip last segment because it's about to move).
    for (let i = 0; i < snake.length - 1; i++) {
      if (snake[i].x === nx && snake[i].y === ny) {
        gameOver();
        return;
      }
    }

    snake.unshift({ x: nx, y: ny });

    if (nx === food.x && ny === food.y) {
      score++;
      stepIntervalMs = Math.max(TICK_MIN_MS, stepIntervalMs - TICK_SPEEDUP);
      food = randomFreeCell();
      beep(660, 0.06);
    } else {
      snake.pop();
    }
  }

  function gameOver() {
    phase = "gameover";
    if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
    beep(120, 0.18);
  }

  function randomFreeCell() {
    while (true) {
      const c = { x: Math.floor(Math.random() * COLS), y: Math.floor(Math.random() * ROWS) };
      if (!snake.some((s) => s.x === c.x && s.y === c.y)) return c;
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    // Background grid.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    ctx.strokeStyle = "rgba(125,249,255,0.04)";
    ctx.lineWidth = 1;
    for (let x = 0; x <= COLS; x++) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(x * CELL, 0); ctx.lineTo(x * CELL, ROWS * CELL);
      ctx.stroke();
    }
    for (let y = 0; y <= ROWS; y++) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(0, y * CELL); ctx.lineTo(COLS * CELL, y * CELL);
      ctx.stroke();
    }

    // Food (pulsing).
    const pulse = 0.85 + 0.15 * Math.sin(performance.now() / 180);
    drawCell(food.x, food.y, `rgba(255,82,174,${pulse.toFixed(3)})`);

    // Snake body.
    for (let i = snake.length - 1; i >= 1; i--) {
      drawCell(snake[i].x, snake[i].y, "#52ffae");
    }
    // Snake head — brighter so the player tracks it instantly.
    drawCell(snake[0].x, snake[0].y, "#9dffd1");

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "gameover") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}` + (score === highScore && score > 0 ? " — new best!" : ""), canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 16);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 44);
    }
  }

  function drawCell(cx, cy, fill) {
    ctx.fillStyle = fill;
    ctx.fillRect(cx * CELL + 1, cy * CELL + 1, CELL - 2, CELL - 2);
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    if (phase !== "playing") return;
    switch (ev.key) {
      case "ArrowUp": case "w": case "W": nextDir = { x: 0, y: -1 }; break;
      case "ArrowDown": case "s": case "S": nextDir = { x: 0, y: 1 }; break;
      case "ArrowLeft": case "a": case "A": nextDir = { x: -1, y: 0 }; break;
      case "ArrowRight": case "d": case "D": nextDir = { x: 1, y: 0 }; break;
    }
  });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    const head = snake[0];
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,                     // "playing" | "gameover"
      score,
      player: {
        x: head.x * CELL + CELL / 2,    // canvas-space center
        y: head.y * CELL + CELL / 2,
        visible: head.x >= 0 && head.x < COLS && head.y >= 0 && head.y < ROWS,
      },
      objective: "Eat the pink food. Don't hit the walls or your own tail.",
    };
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

function update(dt) {
  // lane shift based on keys
  if (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A')) {
    player.lane = (player.lane - 1 + 3) % 3;
    // update y based on lane
    player.y = (player.lane + 0.5) * (canvas.height / 3);
    // optional: remove key to avoid repeat until release - but we don't.
  }
  if (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D')) {
    player.lane = (player.lane + 1) % 3;
    player.y = (player.lane + 0.5) * (canvas.height / 3);
  }

  // forward movement
  player.x += PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
}

Implementation:

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      publishState();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

function update(dt) {
   // Move player forward
   player.x += PLAYER_SPEED * dt;

   // Lane shift based on keys: left/right arrow and A/D or left/right.
   // SHIFT should be immediate per key press: maybe if keys has ArrowLeft, etc.
   // We'll implement: if (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') ... ) shift left one lane and remove key to prevent continuous shift? Usually we handle keydown only, but we may just treat as left push; but later tasks may implement smoothing.
   // Let's implement simple: on each frame, check if keys contains 'ArrowLeft','a','A','ArrowRight','d','D'. If so, shift player.lane appropriately, but only once per press? That's tricky. We'll implement a simple immediate shift: if key is pressed (in keys set) then shift once and remove that key to avoid repeated shift. This is simplistic.

   // Wrap around lane index.
   // Process left shift:
   if (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A')) {
       player.lane = (player.lane + LANE_COUNT - 1) % LANE_COUNT;
       keys.delete('ArrowLeft');
       keys.delete('a');
       keys.delete('A');
   }
   // Process right shift:
   if (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D')) {
       player.lane = (player.lane + 1) % LANE_COUNT;
       keys.delete('ArrowRight');
       keys.delete('d');
       keys.delete('D');
   }

   // Update hazard spawn timer
   hazardSpawnTimer -= dt;
   if (hazardSpawnTimer <= 0) {
        // spawn hazard
        // ensure safe radius: hazard.x = player.x + SAFE_RADIUS + randomExtra
        const extra = Math.random() * 200; // random extra distance
        const lane = Math.floor(Math.random() * LANE_COUNT);
        hazards.push({ lane, x: player.x + SAFE_RADIUS + extra });
        // reset timer
        hazardSpawnTimer = HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL;
   }

   // Move hazards left relative to player? Since we are using world coordinates, we don't need to move them; they are static in world, but we can move them relative to player by subtracting player.x? For later rendering, we may calculate screenX = hazard.x - player.x, or move hazard.x -= HAZARD_SPEED * dt as an alternative.
   // Let's implement hazard.x -= HAZARD_SPEED * dt; This will make them travel towards player.
   for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
        hazards[i].x -= HAZARD_SPEED * dt;
        // Remove hazards that have passed behind the player (x < player.x - something)
        if (hazards[i].x < player.x - 100) {
            hazards.splice(i, 1);
        }
   }
}

```
function update(dt) {
  // Move player forward
  player.x += PLAYER_SPEED * dt;

  // Lane shifting based on keys
  if (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A')) {
    player.lane = (player.lane + LANE_COUNT - 1) % LANE_COUNT;
    keys.delete('ArrowLeft');
    keys.delete('a');
    keys.delete('A');
  }
  if (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D')) {
    player.lane = (player.lane + 1) % LANE_COUNT;
    keys.delete('ArrowRight');
    keys.delete('d');
    keys.delete('D');
  }

  // Hazard spawn timer
  hazardSpawnTimer -= dt;
  if (hazardSpawnTimer <= 0) {
    // Spawn hazard at safe distance ahead of player
    const extra = Math.random() * 200; // random extra offset
    const lane = Math.floor(Math.random() * LANE_COUNT);
    hazards.push({ lane, x: player.x + SAFE_RADIUS + extra });
    // Reset timer
    hazardSpawnTimer = HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  }

  // Move hazards towards player
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    hazards[i].x -= HAZARD_SPEED * dt;
    // Remove hazards that passed behind the player
    if (hazards[i].x < player.x - 100) {
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
    }
  }
}

// === RENDER =============================================================
```

Now implement update(dt):

```
function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== 'playing') return;
  // update safe timer and spawn timer
  safeTimer = Math.max(0, safeTimer - dt);
  hazardSpawnTimer += dt;
  if (safeTimer <= 0 && hazardSpawnTimer >= HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL) {
    spawnHazard();
    hazardSpawnTimer = 0;
  }

  // move hazards
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.y += HAZARD_SPEED * dt;
    // check collision
    if (h.lane === player.lane && h.y + HAZARD_RADIUS >= player.y - PLAYER_RADIUS && h.y - HAZARD_RADIUS <= player.y + PLAYER_RADIUS) {
      // collision
      phase = 'gameover';
      beep(120, 0.18);
      // stop loop? we will stop spawning etc.
      // keep hazard for rendering maybe
      break;
    }
    // if passed player (i.e., beyond bottom)
    if (h.y - HAZARD_RADIUS > canvas.height) {
      // survived
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
      score++;
      beep(660, 0.06);
    }
  }
}
```

Define spawnHazard:

```
function spawnHazard() {
  const lane = Math.floor(Math.random() * LANE_COUNT);
  const hazard = { lane, y: -HAZARD_RADIUS*2 };
  hazards.push(hazard);
}
```

Now define render function:

```
function render() {
  // background
  ctx.fillStyle = '#0f1320';
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
  // lanes
  const laneW = canvas.width / LANE_COUNT;
  ctx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(125,249,255,0.04)';
  ctx.lineWidth = 1;
  for (let i = 1; i < LANE_COUNT; i++) {
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(i * laneW, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(i * laneW, canvas.height);
    ctx.stroke();
  }
  // hazards
  ctx.fillStyle = '#ff5252';
  for (const h of hazards) {
    const hx = h.lane * laneW + laneW/2;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(hx, h.y, HAZARD_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI*2);
    ctx.fill();
  }
  // player
  const px = player.lane * laneW + laneW/2;
  ctx.fillStyle = '#9dffd1';
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(px, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI*2);
  ctx.fill();

  // HUD
  ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
  ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
  ctx.textAlign = 'left';
  ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);

  // game over overlay
  if (phase === 'gameover') {
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(10,10,15,0.7)';
    ctx.fillRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);
    ctx.fillStyle = '#ff52ae';
    ctx.textAlign = 'center';
    ctx.font = '44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillText('Game Over', canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
    ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
    ctx.font = '20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 16);
    ctx.fillStyle = '#a5a5b8';
    ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillText('Press R or click Restart', canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 44);
  }
}
```

```
function reset() {
  player.lane = 1;
  player.x = 0; // not used
  player.y = canvas.height * 0.85; // or canvas.height - 50.
  player.visible = true;
  hazards.length = 0;
  score = 0;
  phase = 'playing';
  lastTime = performance.now();
  hazardSpawnTimer = 0;
  safeTimer = SAFE_RADIUS / PLAYER_SPEED; // which equals 3s but compute from const
  publishState();
  startLoop();

```
function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = 'square';
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.06, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch {}
}
```

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        // Clear canvas each frame
        ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
        // Publish state after each tick (optional but keeps observable up‑to‑date)
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error('loop error', err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
```

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Lane Shift
Concept: The dot auto‑advances forward through a three‑lane tunnel. Each tap instantly shifts it one lane left or right (wrapping around), letting you line up with gaps that appear in different lanes.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.
